| `totalPressureFvPatchScalarField` | custom-kernels | p = p0 on outflow, p0 - dynamic head on inflow; a pos0(phi) select over the dynamicPressure expression. | Registry lookups only. | S | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvPatchFields/derived/totalPressure` |
| `totalTemperatureFvPatchScalarField` | custom-kernels | T = T0/(1 + 0.5(gamma-1)\|U\|^2/(gamma*R*T)) per face - a few flops and one divide. | psi/gamma/U by registry name; the thermo-derived R is a per-face value. | S | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvPatchFields/derived/totalTemperature` |
| `transformFvPatchField` | custom-kernels | psi_b = T & psi_P with a per-face transform tensor; snGradTransformDiag is elementwise. | None. | S | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvPatchFields/basic/transform` |
| `translatingWallVelocityFvPatchVectorField` | custom-kernels | U(t) - n(n&U(t)) with U(t) a host scalar vector per step. | Host Function1 evaluation per step. | S | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvPatchFields/derived/translatingWallVelocity` |
| `transonicEntrainmentPressureFvPatchScalarField` | custom-kernels | Adds the transonic isentropic p0/(1+0.5(gamma-1)M^2)^(gamma/(gamma-1)) branch - one pow per face. | gamma/psi by registry name; pow is the only cost. | S | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvPatchFields/derived/transonicEntrainmentPressure` |
| `turbulentInletFvPatchField` | custom-kernels | Read updateCoeffs: one ranGen_.sample01 per face, then psi = (1-alpha)*psi_old + alpha*(ref + rmsCorr*fluctuationScale*(rand-0.5)*\|ref\|). Despite being a turbulent-inflow condition this is uncorrelated white noise with an AR(1) temporal filter - no spectral synthesis and no FFT anywhere. | None beyond swapping Foam::Random for cuRAND with per-face states; note that changes the random sequence, so it will not bit-match the CPU run. | S | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvPatchFields/derived/turbulentInlet` |
| `turbulentIntensityKineticEnergyFvScalarFieldSource` | custom-kernels | k = 1.5*(I*\|U\|)^2 evaluated on the source cells - one magnitude and a square each. | U fetched by registry name. | S | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvFieldSources/derived/turbulentIntensityKineticEnergy` |
| `turbulentIntensityKineticEnergyInletFvPatchScalarField` | custom-kernels | k = 1.5*(I*\|U\|)^2 with an inletOutlet switch - one magnitude, one square, one select per face. | U and phi by registry name. | S | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvPatchFields/derived/turbulentIntensityKineticEnergyInlet` |
| `uniformDensityHydrostaticPressureFvPatchScalarField` | custom-kernels | p = pRefValue + rhoRef*g&(Cf - pRefPoint) - a dot product per face with constant rhoRef. | None; pRefValue may be a host Function1. | S | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvPatchFields/derived/uniformDensityHydrostaticPressure` |
| `uniformFixedGradientFvPatchField` | custom-kernels | Uniform Function1(t) gradient then the fixedGradient evaluation. | Host Function1 evaluation per step. | S | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvPatchFields/derived/uniformFixedGradient` |
| `uniformFixedValueFvFieldSource` | custom-kernels | A single Function1(t) value broadcast over the source cells - a fill. | Host Function1 evaluation per step (one-way push). | S | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvFieldSources/derived/uniformFixedValue` |
| `uniformFixedValueFvPatchField` | custom-kernels | Fills the patch with one Function1(t) value - a memset-like kernel or just a kernel argument. | Host Function1 evaluation per step (one-way push). | S | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvPatchFields/derived/uniformFixedValue` |
| `uniformInletOutletFvFieldSource` | custom-kernels | Uniform value when the source is positive, the internal cell value when negative - a sign select per source cell. | Host Function1 evaluation per step. | S | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvFieldSources/derived/uniformInletOutlet` |
| `uniformInletOutletFvPatchField` | custom-kernels | inletOutlet with the inlet value from a Function1 of time. | Host Function1 scalar per step pushed as a kernel argument. | S | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvPatchFields/derived/uniformInletOutlet` |
| `uniformJumpFvPatchField` | custom-kernels | fixedJump with the jump from a Function1 of time - one host scalar per step filling the jump array. | Function1 evaluates on host; pushing a scalar as a kernel argument is a one-way transfer, not a stalling round-trip. | S | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvPatchFields/derived/uniformJump` |
| `uniformTotalPressureFvPatchScalarField` | custom-kernels | totalPressure with p0 from a uniform Function1 of time. | Host Function1 evaluation per step. | S | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvPatchFields/derived/uniformTotalPressure` |
| `unweightedLeastSquaresVectors (alternative implementation)` | custom-kernels | Identical structure to leastSquaresVectors with unit weights; not in Make/files so it is a compile-time swap. | None; same kernel with the weight term removed. | S | `src/finiteVolume/finiteVolume/gradSchemes/LeastSquaresGrad/unweightedLeastSquaresVectors.C` |
| `upwind<Type>` | custom-kernels | w = pos0(phi_f), limiter identically zero; no correction kernel. | None; the reference kernel for the whole limited family. | S | `src/finiteVolume/interpolation/surfaceInterpolation/limitedSchemes/upwind` |
| `vanAlbadaLimiter (vanAlbada / vanAlbadaV)` | custom-kernels | r(r+1)/(r^2+1) after clipping r at 0; one divide per face. | None. | S | `src/finiteVolume/interpolation/surfaceInterpolation/limitedSchemes/vanAlbada` |
| `vanLeerLimiter (vanLeer / vanLeerV / limitedVanLeer / vanLeer01)` | custom-kernels | (r+\|r\|)/(1+\|r\|) - branch-free, no divergence, ideal for warps. | None. Port this one first; it is the most-used scheme in practice. | S | `src/finiteVolume/interpolation/surfaceInterpolation/limitedSchemes/vanLeer` |
| `variableHeightFlowRateFvPatchScalarField` | custom-kernels | zeroGradient on outflow clipped to [lowerBound, upperBound], fixed on inflow - a per-face select and clamp. | None; phi by registry name. | S | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvPatchFields/derived/variableHeightFlowRate` |
| `variableHeightFlowRateInletVelocityFvPatchVectorField` | Thrust/CUB | -n*Q*alpha_f/sum(alpha_f*\|Sf\|) - an alpha-weighted patch reduction then an elementwise scale. | Patch reduction per step; alpha by registry name. | S | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvPatchFields/derived/variableHeightFlowRateInletVelocity` |
| `volPointInterpolation` | custom-kernels | psi_point = sum over the point's cells of w*psi_c / sum w - a ragged gather-and-normalise using precomputed inverse-distance weights, i.e. a CSR SpMV from cells to points. | pointCells addressing is a labelListList needing CSR flattening; syncTools::syncPointList is MPI/cyclic point merging (no-op on a single GPU except for cyclic/wedge point pairs); pointConstraints must be applied afterwards. | M | `src/finiteVolume/interpolation/volPointInterpolation` |
| `waveSurfacePressureFvPatchScalarField` | custom-kernels | Read updateCoeffs: dZetap = dt*nf*phi/magSf, integrated in time into the zeta boundary field by an Euler/CN/backward branch, then p = -rho*g&zeta. All elementwise. | It calls zeta.mesh().schemes().ddt(zeta.name()) and then ddtSchemeTypeNames_[name] - a dictionary read plus a HashTable string lookup on every update inside the time loop. It also mutates zeta's boundary field via lookupObjectRef (non-const registry access). | S | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvPatchFields/derived/waveSurfacePressure` |
| `waveTransmissiveFvPatchField` | custom-kernels | advectionSpeed() = phi/(rho*magSf) + sqrt(gamma/psi) - elementwise on the patch, then the advective machinery. | Inherits advective's per-update ddt-scheme string lookup; psi and gamma are fetched from the registry by name. | S | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvPatchFields/derived/waveTransmissive` |
| `wedgeFvPatchField` | custom-kernels | psi_b = cellT & psi_P with a single constant rotation tensor for the whole patch. | None. | S | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvPatchFields/constraint/wedge` |
| `weighted<Type>` | custom-kernels | Uses a surfaceScalarField of weights held by const reference; the interpolation is the standard gather. | The weights field is resolved by objectRegistry name lookup at construction; keep the device pointer, not the name. | S | `src/finiteVolume/interpolation/surfaceInterpolation/schemes/weighted` |
| `zeroGradientFvPatchField` | custom-kernels | psi_b = psi_P, snGrad = 0; a gather and two constant coefficient arrays. | None. | S | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvPatchFields/basic/zeroGradient` |
| `zeroInletOutletFvPatchField` | custom-kernels | inletOutlet with a compile-time zero inlet value - the cheapest variant. | None. | S | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvPatchFields/derived/zeroInletOutlet` |

### B-sparse-solve  <sub>(3)</sub>

| Component | Library | Why | Blockers | Effort | Path |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `fvMatrix<Type>` | AMGX | Assembly (diag/upper/lower/source, internalCoeffs/boundaryCoeffs, negSumDiag, A(), H(), H1(), flux(), relax()) is all flat face/cell arithmetic, but the class's runtime cost is dominated by solve() delegating to lduMatrix::solver. | relax() needs sumMagOffDiag (a face scatter-add) and calls relaxationFactor() which reads the fvSolution dictionary every call inside the time loop. lduMatrix's face-addressed lower/upper storage must be converted to CSR once for AMGX - keep the mapping resident and write coefficients straight into the CSR value array to avoid a per-iteration conversion. | L | `src/finiteVolume/fvMatrices/fvMatrix` |
| `fvMatrix<Type>::fvSolver` | AMGX | Caches an lduMatrix::solver so a matrix can be re-solved per component without re-selecting; exactly the right place to hang a persistent AMGX handle. | lduMatrix::solver::New reads the solvers dictionary (solver/preconditioner/smoother keywords) at construction; the AMGX config must be built once and the solver setup reused across components and timesteps. | M | `src/finiteVolume/fvMatrices/fvMatrix/fvMatrixSolve.C` |
| `fvScalarMatrix (fvMatrix<scalar> specialisation)` | AMGX | The pressure equation lands here; its cost is entirely the sparse solve, which is where a GPU port pays for itself. setComponentReference is a single indexed diag/source update. | Same CSR conversion as fvMatrix. Pressure needs a strong AMG preconditioner - this is the case for installing AMGX rather than relying on cuSPARSE ILU. | L | `src/finiteVolume/fvMatrices/fvScalarMatrix` |

### D-hard  <sub>(19)</sub>

| Component | Library | Why | Blockers | Effort | Path |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `cellPointWeight` | custom-kernels | Read findTetrahedron: builds List<tetIndices> cellTets = polyMeshTetDecomposition::cellTetIndices(mesh, celli) per call, loops tets testing barycentric signs, and on failure runs a second loop finding the nearest tet. | Per-query heap allocation of the tet decomposition, a search loop with early exit, and a fallback nearest-point minimisation. Needs a precomputed flat tet table plus a fixed-trip-count kernel to be viable. | L | `src/finiteVolume/interpolation/interpolation/interpolationCellPoint/cellPointWeight` |
| `faceAreaPairGAMGAgglomeration` | AMGX | Thin wrapper that feeds sqrt(magSf)-derived weights into pairGAMGAgglomeration's greedy sequential pairing, which walks cells in order merging each with its best unmerged neighbour. | Greedy sequential matching is inherently order-dependent and serial; also builds coarse-level addressing with dynamic sizing. Do not port it - AMGX supplies its own GPU aggregation/classical coarsening, so this class simply drops out of the device path. | L | `src/finiteVolume/fvMatrices/solvers/GAMGSymSolver/GAMGAgglomerations/faceAreaPairGAMGAgglomeration` |
| `fvc::smooth / fvc::spread / fvc::sweep` | custom-kernels | Read the source: DynamicList<labelPair> changedPatchAndFaces with .append() per changed face, then FvFaceCellWave<smoothData>::iterate(nLayers) - an irregular front-propagation with a shrinking active set. | Dynamic list growth, a data-dependent active-face frontier, and FvFaceCellWave's MPI face exchange. A device port means rewriting it as a fixed-sweep Jacobi relaxation (different algorithm, different answer at the margins). | L | `src/finiteVolume/finiteVolume/fvc/fvcSmooth/fvcSmooth.H` |
| `interpolationCellPoint<Type>` | custom-kernels | Requires locating the containing tetrahedron of the cell for each query position, then barycentric weighting of cell/face/point values. | Delegates to cellPointWeight, which allocates a List<tetIndices> per query and loops tets until one contains the point - dynamic allocation and a data-dependent search per particle. | L | `src/finiteVolume/interpolation/interpolation/interpolationCellPoint` |
| `interpolationCellPointFace<Type>` | custom-kernels | Searches the cell's tets for the containing one using cell, point and face values, and falls back to the closest tet when the position lies outside all of them. | Same per-query tet search and fallback minimisation as cellPointWeight, with an extra face-value branch. | L | `src/finiteVolume/interpolation/interpolation/interpolationCellPointFace` |
| `interpolationCellPointWallModified<Type>` | custom-kernels | cellPoint plus a wall-face point-field modification that extrapolates and rotates vectors towards the reverse point normal. | Inherits the cellPointWeight tet search; the wall rotation itself is elementwise on the point field. | L | `src/finiteVolume/interpolation/interpolation/interpolationCellPointWallModified` |
| `interpolationPointMVC<Type>` | custom-kernels | Mean Value Coordinates over all vertices of the cell, computed per query position. | Delegates to pointMVCWeight; see below. | L | `src/finiteVolume/interpolation/interpolation/interpolationPointMVC` |
| `mappedFlowRateVelocityFvPatchVectorField` | Thrust/CUB | Reduces the flow rate on the neighbour patch and imposes -n*Q/(rho*A) here; the reduction is trivial but the neighbour access is mapped. | mappedPatchBase addressing plus a cross-region scalar that must reach this patch each step. | M | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvPatchFields/derived/mappedFlowRateVelocity` |
| `mappedInternalValueFvPatchField` | custom-kernels | Reads mapper().map().distribute(sampleValues) or mapper().distribute(nbrField) to pull values from arbitrary interior cells located by a geometric search. | The sample-cell search is host octree/cell-search work; distribute() is a permutation/scatter. Static cases can precompute a device gather map; anything with motion cannot. | L | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvPatchFields/derived/mappedInternalValue` |
| `mappedValueFvPatchField` | custom-kernels | Read the source: mapper().fromNeighbour(nbrPatchField) routes through mappedPatchBase, which owns an AMI/nearest-face map, and it bumps UPstream::msgType() around the call; optional gSum-based average rescaling follows. | mappedPatchBase addressing is built by a host geometric search (nearest cell/face or AMI weights) and its distribute() is an MPI-style scatter even in serial. Flattening it to a static device gather map is possible only if the mapping never changes. | L | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvPatchFields/derived/mappedValue` |
| `mappedVelocityFluxFvPatchField` | custom-kernels | Maps both U and phi from a neighbour patch so the pair stays consistent - two mapped gathers plus a flux rescale. | Same mappedPatchBase search/distribute blockers, doubled. | L | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvPatchFields/derived/mappedVelocityFlux` |
| `movingMappedWallVelocityFvPatchVectorField` | custom-kernels | Takes the wall velocity from the mesh velocity of a neighbouring region through the mapped-patch machinery. | mappedPatchBase search/distribute plus cross-region mesh motion; both host-side. | L | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvPatchFields/derived/movingMappedWallVelocity` |
| `nonConformalCyclicFvPatchField` | custom-kernels | Couples arbitrarily meshed boundary pairs, so the face-to-face weights come from a geometric intersection that is rebuilt whenever the interface moves. | The intersection/stitching is host geometry work with dynamic addressing; the coupling changes size and structure during the run, which breaks a fixed device CSR. This is the main obstacle to a GPU-resident sliding-interface case. | XL | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvPatchFields/constraint/nonConformalCyclic` |
| `nonConformalCyclicFvsPatchField / nonConformalErrorFvsPatchField / nonConformalProcessorCyclicFvsPatchField` | none | Surface-field counterparts of the non-conformal couplings; their sizes track the host-rebuilt intersection. | Patch size and addressing change whenever the interface is re-stitched, invalidating any fixed device layout. | L | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvsPatchFields/constraint/nonConformalCyclic, nonConformalError, nonConformalProcessorCyclic` |
| `nonConformalErrorFvPatchField` | custom-kernels | Holds the unmatched residual area of a non-conformal coupling so the cell geometry stays closed; its size follows the intersection result. | Size and addressing are regenerated by the host intersection each interface update. | L | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvPatchFields/constraint/nonConformalError` |
| `nonConformalMappedPolyFacesFvsPatchLabelField` | none | polyFaces variant for non-conformal mapped inter-region couplings. | Same host-rebuilt addressing, plus a cross-region map. | M | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvsPatchFields/derived/nonConformalMappedPolyFaces` |
| `nonConformalPolyFacesFvsPatchLabelField` | none | polyFaces variant carrying the originating owner-patch information for non-conformal couplings. | Regenerated by the host each time the non-conformal interface is rebuilt, so the label array cannot be treated as static. | M | `src/finiteVolume/fields/fvsPatchFields/derived/nonConformalPolyFaces` |
| `pointMVCWeight` | custom-kernels | Read the source: builds a Map<label> toLocal hash of the cell's points and DynamicList<scalar> alpha/theta and DynamicList<point> u sized 100, then loops every triangle of every face of the cell computing asin/tan per edge. | Per-query hash table construction and three dynamic lists, plus a variable-length face-triangle loop. Not portable without a full rewrite against a flat per-cell vertex table. | XL | `src/finiteVolume/interpolation/interpolation/interpolationPointMVC/pointMVCWeight.H` |
| `smoothData / sweepData` | none | FvFaceCellWave transport types: tiny structs but their updateCell/updateFace methods encode the wave's convergence semantics and are called through the wave driver. | Only meaningful inside FvFaceCellWave; inherit its wavefront blocker. | S | `src/finiteVolume/finiteVolume/fvc/fvcSmooth/smoothData.H, sweepData.H` |

### E-cpu-only  <sub>(45)</sub>

| Component | Library | Why | Blockers | Effort | Path |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CentredFitData<Polynomial>` | none | MeshObject caching FitData's per-face SVD coefficients; setup only. | Inherits FitData's SVD + retry loop. Fine for static meshes; a hard blocker on moving/adapting meshes. | S | `src/finiteVolume/interpolation/surfaceInterpolation/schemes/CentredFitScheme/CentredFitData.H` |
| `CentredFitSnGradData<Polynomial>` | none | Per-face weighted SVD pseudo-inverse of a variable-size Vandermonde matrix with a data-dependent retry loop (weights *=10 up to 8 times). | Variable matrix sizes, per-face convergence branching and SVD - keep on host, compute once, upload the coefficient CSR. | `FitData<Form,ExtendedStencil,Polynomial>` | none | Read calcFit: per-face findFaceDirs, a variable-size weighted Vandermonde, an SVD (svd.VSinvUt()), a goodness test, and up to 8 retries multiplying the central weights by 10 - data-dependent iteration per face. | Variable matrix sizes, per-face convergence branching, SVD, and a linearLimitFactor fallback to plain linear/upwind. Accept a full host round-trip and re-upload; there is nothing to gain from a device port. | M | `src/finiteVolume/interpolation/mapping/fvFieldMappers` |
| `UpwindFitData<Polynomial>` | none | Caches upwind-biased fit coefficients from the same FitData SVD machinery, for both flux directions. | Per-face SVD with retries; host-only precompute. | S | `src/finiteVolume/interpolation/surfaceInterpolation/schemes/UpwindFitScheme/UpwindFitData.H` |

This is the non-conformal configuration compatible with device residency: pay the intersection cost at startup, then treat the non-conformal face addressing as a static device CSR like any other. | low | `src/finiteVolume/fvMesh/fvMeshStitchers/stationary/fvMeshStitchersStationary.H` |
| `fvMeshTopoChangers::none` | none | Fixed topology - update() does nothing. ### B-sparse-solve  <sub>(6)</sub>

| Component | Library | Why | Blockers | Effort | Path |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `displacementComponentLaplacianFvMotionSolver` | AMGX (or cuSPARSE+custom AMG) | One scalar Laplacian solve instead of three - strictly cheaper than the full displacementLaplacian and the same matrix structure. | Same diffusivity and volPointInterpolation blockers, plus a component-index gather/scatter between the scalar cellDisplacement component and the vector point field. Because it is used for 2D/extruded meshes, twoDCorrectPoints must also run on device (a masked component zeroing - trivial). | high | `src/fvMotionSolver/fvMotionSolvers/componentDisplacement/componentLaplacian/displacementComponentLaplacianFvMotionSolver.H` |
| `displacementLaplacianFvMotionSolver` | AMGX (or cuSPARSE+custom AMG) | solve() is Foam::solve(fvm::laplacian(gamma, cellDisplacement)) - a symmetric scalar-coefficient Laplacian assembled on the standard lduMatrix, solved 3x (one per component). This is the canonical AMGX target and the single most valuable port in the whole subsystem. | Three things sit around the solve. (1) diffusivity().correct() then diffusivity()::operator()() is called EVERY solve - for inverseDistance/inversePointDistance that is a full distance wave per time step (see those entries). (2) curPoints() calls volPointInterpolation::interpolate(cellDisplacement_, pointDisplacement_) which uses static pointCells+pointWeights CSR (device-friendly gather) but then addSeparated()/pushUntransformedData() do per-patch MPI exchange and cyclic transforms. (3) frozenPointsZone_ is a scatter over a static pointZone - trivial. topoChange()/mapMesh() clear diffusivityPtr_ forcing full rebuild. | high | `src/fvMotionSolver/fvMotionSolvers/displacement/laplacian/displacementLaplacianFvMotionSolver.H` |
| `displacementSBRStressFvMotionSolver` | AMGX + custom CUDA kernels | Confirmed in the .C: fvm::laplacian(2*Df, D) plus an explicit fvc::div of Df*(dotInterpolate(Sf, gradCd.T()-gradCd) - Sf*interpolate(tr(gradCd))). The implicit part is the same lduMatrix Laplacian; the explicit part is a volTensorField gradient (fvc::grad, a face-loop reduction into cells) plus interpolation and divergence. | Heavier than plain Laplacian: needs a device fvc::grad producing a full volTensorField (9 components x nCells - watch 16 GB on large meshes), device dotInterpolate and fvc::div. All are standard face-gather/cell-scatter patterns with atomics or a face-owner CSR, but it is three more kernels than the Laplacian case. Same diffusivity and volPointInterpolation blockers. | high | `src/fvMotionSolver/fvMotionSolvers/displacement/SBRStress/displacementSBRStressFvMotionSolver.H` |
| `rigidBodyMeshMotionSolver` | AMGX (or cuSPARSE+custom AMG) | Applies the body transforms as pointDisplacement BOUNDARY values and delegates the interior deformation to a nested motion solver - in practice displacementLaplacian. So the dominant cost is a sparse Laplacian solve, not the rigid-body algebra. | Inherits everything from the nested solver: per-solve diffusivity re-evaluation (D-hard if inverseDistance-family) and volPointInterpolation's per-patch MPI. Plus the same per-step forces construction as rigidBodyMeshMotion. Better mesh quality than the direct interpolating variant, at the cost of a linear solve per step. | high | `src/rigidBodyMotion/rigidBodyMeshMotion/rigidBodyMeshMotionSolver/rigidBodyMeshMotionSolver.H` |
| `velocityComponentLaplacianFvMotionSolver` | AMGX (or cuSPARSE+custom AMG) | Single scalar Laplacian on cellMotionU component. Same AMGX path. | As displacementComponentLaplacian. | high | `src/fvMotionSolver/fvMotionSolvers/componentVelocity/componentLaplacian/velocityComponentLaplacianFvMotionSolver.H` |
| `velocityLaplacianFvMotionSolver` | AMGX (or cuSPARSE+custom AMG) | Identical structure to the displacement variant: fvm::laplacian(gamma, cellMotionU) = 0, then interpolate to points and x += U*dt. Same lduMatrix -> AMGX path. | Same volPointInterpolation MPI/patch path and same per-solve diffusivity re-evaluation as displacementLaplacian. Note motionDirectionalDiffusivity looks up 'cellMotionU' by name from the registry, so if paired with it the diffusivity depends on the previous solution - keep that field on device to avoid a round-trip. | high | `src/fvMotionSolver/fvMotionSolvers/velocity/laplacian/velocityLaplacianFvMotionSolver.H` |

### D-hard  <sub>(18)</sub>

| Component | Library | Why | Blockers | Effort | Path |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MeshToMeshMapGeometricFields` | cuSPARSE (SpMM) or custom CUDA kernels | Template machinery applying the SHARED src->tgt addressing and weights to every registered vol/surface/point field. Once the weights exist, this is a sparse gather-and-weighted-sum - structurally an SpMV/SpMM per field. | The weights come from meshToMesh's host-side overlap computation, so they must be uploaded at each mapping event. It iterates the objectRegistry by type to find fields (host HashTable traversal) and reallocates each field to the new size. Worth porting only if mapping events are frequent enough to matter; the addressing build dominates anyway. | high | `src/fvMeshTopoChangers/meshToMesh/MeshToMeshMapGeometricFields.H` |
| `displacementLayeredMotionMotionSolver` | custom CUDA kernels (frontier BFS) + Thrust | The layer coordinate comes from PointEdgeWave<pointEdgeStructuredWalk> (line 191 of the .C): an irregular, iteration-count-unknown frontier propagation over pointEdges/edgePoints with syncTools::syncPointList and syncEdgeList inside the loop. The blend itself is elementwise, but the walk is a graph BFS. | PointEdgeWave is a serial worklist with MPI sync per sweep. A DynamicList<label> meshPoints accumulation at line 365 and forAllConstIter over the patches dictionary at 310/357 are host-only dictionary parsing. The saving grace: for a fixed cellZone/faceZone topology the walk result is time-INVARIANT, so it can be computed once on the host at setup and uploaded as a static per-point blend weight. Only the faceZone boundary displacement then needs per-step device work. | medium | `src/motionSolvers/displacement/layeredSolver/displacementLayeredMotionMotionSolver.H` |
| `fvMeshMover` | custom CUDA kernels | This is the real hinge for device residency, and it is not visible in the header. Every mover ends in mesh().movePoints(p), and I traced what that does: fvMesh::movePoints stores V0/V00, calls polyMesh::movePoints -> primitiveMesh::movePoints which computes sweptVols per face via f[facei].sweptVol(oldPoints, newPoints), then clearGeom(), then updateGeomNotOldVol() (face centres/areas via face::areaAndCentre, cell centres/volumes), then surfaceInterpolation::movePoints() and meshObjects::movePoints. meshPhi = sweptVols/deltaT. | Three concrete problems. (1) The per-face kernels iterate faceList = List<face>, where each face owns its own labelList - NOT a flat buffer. You must flatten to a CSR face-point array once per topology; the faces are static under pure motion, so this is a one-time cost. (2) polyMesh::movePoints also does bounds_ = boundBox(points_) (a global reduction), globalMeshDataPtr_().movePoints (MPI), clears cellTreePtr_, and resets geometricD_/solutionD_. (3) meshObjects::movePoints fans out to every registered mesh object - each needs its own device movePoints. Good news: sweptVol, areaAndCentre and cellCentresAndVols are all embarrassingly parallel per face/cell with no addressing change, and velocityMotionCorrection::update() is just correctBoundaryConditions on named U fields. | high | `src/finiteVolume/fvMesh/fvMeshMovers/fvMeshMover/fvMeshMover.H` |
| `fvMeshMovers::motionSolver` | custom CUDA kernels | update() is three lines: mesh().movePoints(motionPtr_->newPoints()); velocityMotionCorrection_.update(). All the cost is in the delegated motion solver plus the fvMesh::movePoints geometry rebuild. | newPoints() currently hands back a host tmp<pointField>. Making this device-resident means changing the motionSolver interface (see motionSolver base) AND porting the movePoints geometry chain (see fvMeshMover). Tier is set by the movePoints chain, not by this class. | high | `src/fvMeshMovers/motionSolver/fvMeshMoversMotionSolver.H` |
| `fvMeshMovers::multiValveEngine` | custom CUDA kernels (frontier BFS) | Per-point displacement is d = w(dist)*motion(t) - elementwise once w exists. But w comes from a patch-to-object distance built with PointEdgeWave/pistonPointEdgeData, and the class builds labelHashSets of moving/sliding/static patches (findSlidingPatchSet, findStaticPatchSet in the .C). | The saving grace is real: movingObject caches the scale field and only recomputes it every travelInterval_ of the stroke, not every step. So the wave is amortised. Between rebuilds the per-step work is a pure per-point scale*translation kernel (movingObject.C line ~130) and is device-friendly. The rebuilds are host PointEdgeWave + syncTools + labelHashSet work and force a scale-field re-upload. Sliding patches additionally keep nodes on the liner/NCC surfaces, which drags in the stitcher. | high | `src/fvMeshMovers/multiValveEngine/multiValveEngine.H` |
| `inverseDistanceDiffusivity` | custom CUDA kernels (frontier BFS) | Confirmed in the .C: operator()() runs fvPatchDistWave::calculate(mesh, patchSet, -vGreat, y) on EVERY call, and the Laplacian solvers call operator()() every solve. That is a full FaceCellWave cell-distance propagation per time step - the dominant non-solve cost. | FaceCellWave is a serial worklist with per-sweep MPI exchange and an unbounded iteration count. mesh().boundaryMesh().patchSet(patchNames_) builds a labelHashSet from word lookups per call. Options: (a) recompute the distance only every N steps, (b) rewrite as a GPU multi-source BFS / eikonal sweep, (c) switch the case to uniform/file diffusivity. Do NOT leave this as-is in a device-resident loop. | high | `src/fvMotionSolver/motionDiffusivity/inverseDistance/inverseDistanceDiffusivity.H` |
| `inverseFaceDistanceDiffusivity` | custom CUDA kernels (frontier BFS) | Same per-call fvPatchDistWave::calculate as inverseDistance, but on a surfaceScalarField, plus a forAllConstIter over the labelHashSet patch IDs writing patch face values from mag(Cf - C) to avoid divide-by-zero. | Identical wave blocker plus the per-patch host fixup loop. The patch fixup itself is a trivial device kernel over patch faces once the wave result is on device. | high | `src/fvMotionSolver/motionDiffusivity/inverseFaceDistance/inverseFaceDistanceDiffusivity.H` |
| `inversePointDistanceDiffusivity` | custom CUDA kernels (frontier BFS) | Worst of the three. Confirmed in the .C: allocates List<pointEdgeDist> over nPoints AND nEdges, seeds by iterating a labelHashSet of patches and testing pointWallDist[pointi].valid(pointEdgeData) per mesh point, then runs a full PointEdgeWave bounded by globalData().nTotalPoints() iterations - all inside operator()(), i.e. every solve. | labelHashSet iteration + per-point valid() seeding + PointEdgeWave with MPI sync per sweep, then a point->face averaging pass. Two large temporary Lists allocated per call. Same three options as inverseDistance; this is the strongest argument for pinning a case to uniform or file diffusivity. | high | `src/fvMotionSolver/motionDiffusivity/inversePointDistance/inversePointDistanceDiffusivity.H` |
| `meshPhiCorrectInfo` | custom CUDA kernels (frontier BFS) | FaceCellWave datum distributing the cell volume-conservation error away from the interface, accumulating face flux corrections per layer. | Same wave-engine dependency as meshPhiPreCorrectInfo, and additionally it is order-sensitive: the redistribution is conservative only if layers are processed in wave order, which a naive parallel relaxation will not reproduce. Would need a level-synchronous formulation. | high | `src/fvMeshStitchers/moving/meshPhiCorrectInfo.H` |
| `meshPhiPreCorrectInfo` | custom CUDA kernels (frontier BFS) | A FaceCellWave datum carrying (layer index, weight) outward from the non-conformal interface. Per-update arithmetic is a trivial layer increment and weight accumulation. | Only meaningful inside FvFaceCellWave, which is a serial worklist with MPI sync. Porting the datum without porting the wave engine buys nothing. | medium | `src/fvMeshStitchers/moving/meshPhiPreCorrectInfo.H` |
| `multiValveEngine::movingObject` | custom CUDA kernels (frontier BFS) | Owns the cached per-point scale_ field: scale = 1 - d1/(d1+d2+rootVSmall), optionally cosine-shaped, then pointConstraints::New(pMesh).constrain(scale_). The shaping arithmetic is elementwise; the d1/d2 distances come from a wave. | calcScale is host (wave + syncTools + pointConstraints MPI) but runs only every travelInterval_. mapMesh resets scale_ to Zero and the scale field is resized by the meshToMesh mapper - i.e. any topology change invalidates the device copy. Per-step application is a clean kernel. | high | `src/fvMeshMovers/multiValveEngine/multiValveEngine.H` |
| `multiValveEngine::pistonObject` | custom CUDA kernels | Piston specialisation of movingObject: axial translation from a Function1 (usually crankConnectingRodMotion) applied through the cached scale field. The translation is O(1) host; the application is a kernel. | piston.C iterates labelHashSets of patch IDs to assemble its patch sets (construction-time). Cylinder-liner sliding keeps nodes on the liner, coupling this to the non-conformal stitcher. Clearance reporting is a small reduction with a host print - a sync point per step unless deferred. | high | `src/fvMeshMovers/multiValveEngine/multiValveEngine.H` |
| `multiValveEngine::valveObject / valveList` | custom CUDA kernels | Valve translation = lift(t) along the valve axis through the cached scale field. Same amortised-wave structure as the piston. | minLift closure detection is a host branch per valve per step (O(1), fine). The real issue is that opening/closing a valve is exactly when the mesh topology and the non-conformal interfaces change - which drops you into the stitcher/topoChanger host path. | high | `src/fvMeshMovers/multiValveEngine/multiValveEngine.H` |
| `pairPatchAgglomeration` | custom CUDA kernels (parallel matching) | Confirmed in the .C: greedy pairwise face agglomeration driven by a HashSet<edge, Hash<edge>> fineFeaturedFaces(coarsePatch.nEdges()/10) and a do/while(!agglomOK) retry loop inside a while(nCreatedLevels < maxLevels_) loop. Builds restrict/prolong operators per level for view-factor/radiation exchange. | Greedy sequential matching by largest shared edge weight is order-dependent - a parallel maximal matching gives a DIFFERENT (though valid) hierarchy, so results will not bit-match. Edge-keyed hash set and the retry loop are host constructs. Mitigating factor: it runs at setup for a fixed patch, so on a static mesh the hierarchy is built once and the per-step restrict/prolong is a static sparse gather that IS device-friendly. Port the operators, leave the construction on host. | high | `src/fvAgglomerationMethods/pairPatchAgglomeration/pairPatchAgglomeration.H` |
| `pistonPointEdgeData` | custom CUDA kernels (frontier BFS) | A PointEdgeWave transported datum carrying nearest-wall-point and squared distance. Only worth it as part of a GPU multi-source BFS/nearest-point rewrite, and even then it is amortised over travelInterval_ so the payoff is small. | medium | `src/fvMeshMovers/multiValveEngine/pistonPointEdgeData/pistonPointEdgeData.H` |
| `pointEdgeStructuredWalk` | custom CUDA kernels | A PointEdgeWave transported datum (point, dist, data) with updatePoint/updateEdge predicates. Its updatePoint carries a branchy 'valid()' guard that maps badly to a lock-step warp. Only worth touching if you rewrite the wave as a GPU BFS. | medium | `src/motionSolvers/displacement/layeredSolver/pointEdgeStructuredWalk.H` |
| `surfaceDisplacementPointPatchVectorField` | custom CUDA kernels (BVH traversal) | Confirmed in the .C: calls surfaces().findNearest(...) at two sites (lines ~120 and ~163) against a searchableSurfaces set. That is recursive indexedOctree traversal over a triSurface, per patch point, per time step. | indexedOctree is a pointer-chasing recursive tree - the single worst pattern in this catalogue for GPU. Porting means replacing it with a flattened BVH and an iterative stack traversal (LBVH-style), which is a real project. POINTNORMAL/FIXEDNORMAL modes add ray-cast queries on top. Also does velocity clipping, optional wedgePlane component removal and a frozenPointsZone scatter (both trivial). Patch-sized work, so if the wetted patch is small a host-side octree query with a small D2H/H2D of the patch points may be the pragmatic answer - but it IS a host round-trip every step. | very high | `src/fvMotionSolver/pointPatchFields/derived/surfaceDisplacement/surfaceDisplacementPointPatchVectorField.H` |
| `surfaceSlipDisplacementPointPatchVectorField` | custom CUDA kernels (BVH traversal) | Same searchableSurface projection machinery as surfaceDisplacement, but re-evaluated on every call so points keep sliding along the triSurface - so the octree query frequency is if anything higher. | Identical indexedOctree recursion blocker. Because the displacement is recomputed rather than accumulated, you cannot amortise the search across steps. | very high | `src/fvMotionSolver/pointPatchFields/derived/surfaceSlipDisplacement/surfaceSlipDisplacementPointPatchVectorField.H` |

This is the hook to inject a GPU-specific ordering (e.g. one tuned for warp-level coalescing or for AMGX aggregation) computed by external tooling. | not worth it | `src/renumber/renumberMethods/manualRenumber/manualRenumber.H` |

| `processorRunTimes` | n/a (host) | Holds the complete-case Time plus one Time per processorN directory for the decompose/reconstruct tools. Filesystem and time-directory bookkeeping. | Utility-time only. No numerics. | not worth it | `src/parallel/parallel/processorRunTimes.H` |
| `randomRenumber` | n/a (host) | Random shuffle, diagnostic only. Preprocessing. | None - but note it will actively DESTROY GPU memory locality, so it is a useful negative control when benchmarking the effect of ordering on SpMV throughput. | not worth it | `src/renumber/renumberMethods/randomRenumber/randomRenumber.H` |
| `refinementHistoryConstraint` | n/a (host) | Confirmed in the .C: looks up or READS polyMesh/refinementHistory from disk, then uses the hexRef8 octree sibling groups as indivisible decomposition units. | File I/O plus a tree structure (splitCells) traversal. Preprocessing only - but note it exists specifically to support the refiner, which is itself E-cpu-only. | not worth it | `src/parallel/decompose/decompositionMethods/decompositionConstraints/refinementHistory/refinementHistoryConstraint.H` |
| `renumberMethod` | n/a (host) | Base for bandwidth-reducing cell reordering. Runs in the renumberMesh UTILITY, before the solver starts - it is not in the time loop at all. | None for device residency, because it never executes during the run. Strategically it is the OPPOSITE of a blocker: a good renumbering directly improves GPU SpMV cache locality, so run it as preprocessing. Do not port; do use. | not worth it | `src/renumber/renumberMethods/renumberMethod/renumberMethod.H` |
| `springRenumber` | n/a (host) | Iterative relaxation of cell indices toward the mean neighbour index, with a sortedOrder(position, shuffle) call per iteration (line 163). Preprocessing only. | The per-iteration global sort is the serial part. Not in the time loop. Ironically the relaxation itself would parallelise well, but there is no reason to bother. | not worth it | `src/renumber/renumberMethods/springRenumber/springRenumber.H` |
| `structuredRenumber` | n/a (host) | Walks mesh layers from named patches with OppositeFaceCellWave, then orders column-first or layer-first. Preprocessing only. | FaceCellWave-based, serial. Not in the time loop. For extruded/boundary-layer meshes depthFirst ordering can meaningfully help GPU locality along columns - worth trying as preprocessing. | not worth it | `src/renumber/renumberMethods/structuredRenumber/structuredRenumber.H` |
| `timeVaryingMappedFixedValuePointPatchField` | n/a (host) | Reads external time-stamped boundaryData point clouds from disk, builds a spatial mapping from that cloud to the patch points, then interpolates in time. | Both file I/O and the spatial mapping construction (nearest/inverse-distance from an external cloud, typically octree- or AMI-backed) are host. The mapping weights are static once built, so the per-step part reduces to a sparse gather that COULD go on device; the reload at each data time cannot. | medium | `src/fvMotionSolver/pointPatchFields/derived/timeVaryingMappedFixedValue/timeVaryingMappedFixedValuePointPatchField.H` |
| `uniformInterpolatedDisplacementPointPatchVectorField` | n/a (host) | Reads stored pointVectorFields out of the time directories and interpolates between the two bracketing ones. Filesystem I/O inside the time loop, same as dynamicMeshPointInterpolator. | Disk read + objectRegistry caching per interval. The blend itself is a 2-term axpy. Prefetching the whole motion history to device is possible only when it fits in 16 GB; otherwise this is an unavoidable host round-trip, though a patch-sized one. | medium | `src/fvMotionSolver/pointPatchFields/derived/uniformInterpolatedDisplacement/uniformInterpolatedDisplacementPointPatchVectorField.H` |

## Mesh: Mesh generation and manipulation

### A-trivial  <sub>(33)</sub>

| Component | Library | Why | Blockers | Effort | Path |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `BSpline` | custom CUDA kernel | BSpline.C, 139 lines, local basis-matrix form with reflected end tangents. No containers, no search, no iteration. | None. | none — off the time loop | `src/mesh/blockMesh/blockEdges/BSplineEdge/BSpline.H` |
| `BSplineEdge` | custom CUDA kernel | Same shape as splineEdge with the uniform cubic B-spline basis matrix (1/6 weights). Elementwise in lambda. | Virtual dispatch through blockEdge. | none — off the time loop | `src/mesh/blockMesh/blockEdges/BSplineEdge/BSplineEdge.H` |
| `CatmullRomSpline` | custom CUDA kernel | CatmullRomSpline.C is 139 lines of closed-form cubic evaluation; the reflected end control points are computed once at construction. | None; work volume is trivial. | none — off the time loop | `src/mesh/blockMesh/blockEdges/splineEdge/CatmullRomSpline.H` |
| `arcEdge` | custom CUDA kernel | arcEdge.C computes the circumcentre/axis/radius once in the constructor (a fixed 3x3 solve), then each position(lambda) is centre + r1*cos(t) + (axis^r1)*sin(t) + axis*length*lambda — pure per-lambda trig with no branching. | Virtual dispatch only; constructor-time centre calculation is scalar host work on 3 points. | none — off the time loop | `src/mesh/blockMesh/blockEdges/arcEdge/arcEdge.H` |
| `badQualityToCell` | custom CUDA kernel + CUB stream compaction | This is the good case the brief distinguishes: it only READS a static mesh. The underlying metrics (non-orthogonality, skewness, face weight, volume ratio, determinant, tet quality, twist, flatness, concavity) are all elementwise geometry over unchanging owner/neighbour/points addressing — a natural per-face kernel followed by a compaction. | badQualityToCell.C:44-58 materialises the result as a faceSet (HashSet), calls faces.sync(mesh) for MPI agreement, then iterates the set with forAllConstIter to mark owner/neighbour. meshCheck::checkMesh itself lives in src/meshTools and is written entirely against host faceSet — porting means reimplementing it, not wrapping it. | M (1-2 wk) to reimplement the metric kernels device-side; worth it only if a device quality check is needed in an AMR loop | `src/mesh/snappyHexMesh/motionSmoother/badQualityToCell/badQualityToCell.H` |
| `badQualityToFace` | custom CUDA kernel + CUB stream compaction | Identical to badQualityToCell but returns the offending faces directly, skipping the owner/neighbour expansion. Read-only over static mesh geometry. | Same faceSet/HashSet materialisation and the same host-only meshCheck::checkMesh dependency in src/meshTools. | M (1-2 wk), shared with badQualityToCell | `src/mesh/snappyHexMesh/motionSmoother/badQualityToFace/badQualityToFace.H` |
| `block` | custom CUDA kernel | blockCreate.C createPoints() is a perfectly independent triple loop over (i,j,k) writing points_[pointLabel(i,j,k)] from 12 precomputed edge-weight arrays — an elementwise transfinite blend with no cross-point dependency, no container growth, no search. It is the single most trivially-parallel routine in the whole subsystem. | Nothing structural in the blend itself. The optional trailing curved-face correction calls correctFacePoints -> projectFace, which is octree-bound (see projectFace). Runs once, so the port buys nothing. | S (~1-2 d) if ever wanted; realistically none — off the time loop | `src/mesh/blockMesh/blocks/block/block.H` |
| `calcSnapDistance` | custom CUDA kernel | snapDistance(p) = snapTol * max(length of edges attached to p) — a max-reduction over the fixed patch point-edge addressing. Pure read of static geometry, exactly the easy half of the mesh question. | Edge-to-point scatter needs atomicMax or a CSR transpose; syncTools point sync on coupled patches for the parallel max. | S (~1-2 d); none needed today | `src/mesh/snappyHexMesh/snappyHexMeshDriver/snappySnapDriver.C` |
| `calculateLayerThickness` | custom CUDA kernel | total = first*(r^n - 1)/(r - 1) evaluated per patch point, with the expansion ratio recovered from total/first by a bounded Newton iteration in layerExpansionRatio. Closed form plus a short fixed-iteration solve, fully independent per point. | relativeSizes scales by the local cell size, which needs the cell-level field; the five layerSpecification branches would become a compile-time or uniform switch. | S (~2 d); none needed today | `src/mesh/snappyHexMesh/snappyHexMeshDriver/snappyLayerDriver.C` |
| `cyclicSector` | custom CUDA kernel | Point map is identical to sector — trivially parallel. What it adds is patch metadata, not arithmetic. | The front/back cyclic patch pair, its rotational transform and the neighbourPatch coupling are host polyPatch/dictionary construction, not a kernel; that half never leaves the CPU. | none — off the time loop | `src/mesh/extrudeModel/cyclicSector/cyclicSector.H` |
| `cylindricalRadial` | custom CUDA kernel | Decomposes p about (axisPt, axis), replaces the radial magnitude by R(layer), keeps the axial component. Elementwise vector algebra. | R is a Function1<scalar> — an RTS virtual object evaluated on the host. It is a function of layer only, so it collapses to an nLayers-entry table that can be uploaded once. | S (~1 d) with the Function1 pre-tabulated; none today | `src/mesh/extrudeModel/cylindricalRadial/cylindricalRadial.H` |
| `danglingCellRefine` | custom CUDA kernel | Unlike every other refinement criterion in this group it does no geometric search at all: it counts, per cell, how many of its faces belong to a finer neighbour and refines when that count exceeds nFaces. That is a face-gather plus a per-cell reduction over static addressing. | Consumed by hexRef8, which rebuilds the mesh on the host; the nAllowRefine budget in the surrounding driver is still order-dependent. | S (~2 d) as a standalone kernel; none in practice | `src/mesh/snappyHexMesh/snappyHexMeshDriver/snappyRefineDriver.C` |
| `edgeVertex` | custom CUDA kernel | The encoding itself is a handful of inline integer ops — vertex v -> v, edge e -> -(e+1), with isEdge/getEdge/getVertex and a coord(weight) lerp. That core would port verbatim and is exactly the kind of packed-index trick a GPU cut representation would want. | edgeVertex.C (257 lines) also carries 4 Map and 4 HashSet helper sites for translating cut sets, and it holds a polyMesh reference — so the class as written is host-bound even though its arithmetic is not. | S (<1 d) for the encoding; the helpers stay on the host | `src/polyTopoChange/meshCut/edgeVertex/edgeVertex.H` |
| `handleFeatureAngle` | custom CUDA kernel | Per patch edge, compare the angle between the two adjacent face normals against featureAngle (convex) or concaveAngle (concave) and unmark. One dot product and two comparisons per edge over fixed patch addressing. | The unmark is a scatter to the two edge points (atomicAnd/atomicMin); syncTools::syncEdgeList for coupled patch edges. | S (~1 d); none needed today | `src/mesh/snappyHexMesh/snappyHexMeshDriver/snappyLayerDriver.C` |
| `handleWarpedFaces / detectWarpedFaces` | custom CUDA kernel | Unmark where layer thickness / face size exceeds maxFaceThicknessRatio — a per-face ratio test against two arrays that already exist. | Scatter of the unmark to the face's points needs atomics; parallel sync on coupled faces. | S (~1 d); none needed today | `src/mesh/snappyHexMesh/snappyHexMeshDriver/snappyLayerDriver.C` |
| `lineDivide` | custom CUDA kernel | lineDivide.C:96-115 is closed form: divisions_[i] = secStart + blockFrac*(1 - g^(i-secnStart+1))/(1 - g^secnDiv) with g = expRatio^(1/(n-1)), or the uniform branch. Per-division independent given the per-section start, which is O(nSections<=3) host scalar work. | The per-section remainder assignment (secnDivs[secnMaxDivs] += nDiv - sumSecnDivs, line 78) is a tiny serial fixup; the final points_ = cedge.position(divisions_) inherits whatever tier the edge is. | none — off the time loop | `src/mesh/blockMesh/blockEdges/lineDivide/lineDivide.H` |
| `lineEdge` | custom CUDA kernel | p(lambda) = p0 + lambda*(p1-p0). Closed form, elementwise over the lambda list, no state. | Only the virtual dispatch through blockEdge; the math itself is two FMAs. | none — off the time loop (would be trivial if it mattered) | `src/mesh/blockMesh/blockEdges/lineEdge/lineEdge.H` |
| `linearDirection` | custom CUDA kernel | p = surfacePoint + dHat*thickness*sumThickness(layer) with dHat and thickness fixed at construction — one FMA per point. | Virtual dispatch only. | none — off the time loop | `src/mesh/extrudeModel/linearDirection/linearDirection.H` |
| `linearNormal` | custom CUDA kernel | linearNormal.C:96-111: return surfacePoint + layerPoints_[layer-1]*surfaceNormal. layerPoints_ is a small scalarList precomputed in the constructor. Elementwise, no branches beyond layer==0. | Virtual dispatch through extrudeModel; layerPoints_ would need to be a device array. | S (~1 d) if a device extruder is ever built; none today | `src/mesh/extrudeModel/linearNormal/linearNormal.H` |
| `linearRadial` | custom CUDA kernel | rs = mag(p); r = rs + (R-rs)*sumThickness(layer); p_new = r*p/mag(p). One rsqrt and a scale per point. | Virtual dispatch only. | none — off the time loop | `src/mesh/extrudeModel/linearRadial/linearRadial.H` |
| `plane (planeExtrusion)` | custom CUDA kernel | linearNormal with nLayers=1; one offset per point. | Empty front/back patch creation is host work. | none — off the time loop | `src/mesh/extrudeModel/planeExtrusion/planeExtrusion.H` |
| `polyLine` | custom CUDA kernel (Thrust inclusive_scan) | polyLine.C builds param_[i] as a cumulative sum of chord lengths normalised by the total — literally a prefix sum followed by a divide, then a binary-search lookup. | None algorithmically; the arrays are O(10) control points. | none — off the time loop | `src/mesh/blockMesh/blockEdges/polyLineEdge/polyLine.H` |
| `polyLineEdge` | custom CUDA kernel | Piecewise-linear evaluation against a precomputed cumulative-chord table — a binary search plus a lerp per lambda, i.e. a gather. Fully independent per point. | Virtual dispatch; the control-point list is a small host pointField built at parse time. | none — off the time loop | `src/mesh/blockMesh/blockEdges/polyLineEdge/polyLineEdge.H` |
| `preSmoothPatch / smoothPatchDisplacement` | custom CUDA kernel (atomics or CSR transpose) | nSmoothPatch sweeps of edge-weighted Laplacian averaging with 1/\|edge\| weights over fixed patch addressing — a Jacobi-style gather/scatter, no linear solve, no topology change. | Edge-scatter accumulation needs atomics; syncTools::syncPointList (MPI) after every sweep; feature/multi-patch constrained points are held in host sets that gate which points move. | S (~2-3 d); none needed today | `src/mesh/snappyHexMesh/snappyHexMeshDriver/snappySnapDriver.C` |
| `sector` | custom CUDA kernel | sector.C (122 lines): p_new = axisPt + Ra(theta)&(p-axisPt) with theta fixed per layer, so the rotation tensor Ra is one 3x3 per layer, precomputable, and the per-point work is a single tensor-vector product. | Virtual dispatch; the per-layer rotation tensors need uploading (nLayers x 9 scalars). | none — off the time loop | `src/mesh/extrudeModel/sector/sector.H` |
| `setNumLayers / growNoExtrusion` | custom CUDA kernel | pointNLayers = max over surrounding faces (a gather-max), then nGrow sweeps of dilation of the NOEXTRUDE set (a Jacobi-style stencil over patch connectivity). Both are standard data-parallel patterns on static addressing. | atomicMax for the face-to-point reduction; syncTools point sync after every dilation sweep; nGrow is small so kernel launch overhead would dominate. | S (~1-2 d); none needed today | `src/mesh/snappyHexMesh/snappyHexMeshDriver/snappyLayerDriver.C` |
| `sigmaRadial` | custom CUDA kernel | p_lay = pRef - layer*(pRef-pStrat)/nLayers; r = mag(x) - (RT/g)*log(p_lay/pRef); p_new = r*xHat. | `smoothDisplacement` | custom CUDA kernel (atomics or CSR transpose) | nSmoothDispl sweeps of edge-weighted Laplacian smoothing of the pointDisplacement field with 1/\|edge\| weights on unchanged mesh addressing. Explicit sweeps, not an implicit solve — no matrix is ever assembled. | Edge-scatter needs atomics; syncTools::syncPointList per sweep; the field is a pointVectorField with boundary conditions applied on the host. | S (~2-3 d); none needed today | `src/mesh/snappyHexMesh/snappyHexMeshDriver/snappySnapDriver.C` |
| `smoothField / smoothPatchNormals / smoothNormals / averageNeighbours` | custom CUDA kernel (atomics or CSR transpose) | snappyLayerDriverShrink.C:43-96 sumWeights accumulates 1/\|e\| into invSumWeight[e[0]] and [e[1]] over the patch edge list, then averageNeighbours does the matching weighted gather. | `sphericalRadial` | custom CUDA kernel | p_new = R(layer) * p/mag(p) — the simplest model in the set; the surface normal is not even used. | Same Function1<scalar> host evaluation; pre-tabulate per layer. | none — off the time loop | `src/mesh/extrudeModel/sphericalRadial/sphericalRadial.H` |

| `wedge` | custom CUDA kernel | sector with nLayers=1 and +/- angle/2 — two rotations applied to the source patch points. Both are min-distance frontier propagations, which map onto a GPU frontier well in principle. | PointEdgeWave is a host iterative driver with changed-point/edge lists, a global reduce per iteration and MPI point sync; seeding logic at lines 1057-1097 is branchy per edge; nMedialAxisIter caps but does not remove the iteration. | L (1-2 mo); none for the time loop | `src/mesh/snappyHexMesh/snappyHexMeshDriver/snappyLayerDriverShrink.C` |

The type is trivial POD; the propagation is a frontier walk over patch edges and faces. | PatchEdgeFaceWave is a host iterative driver with changed-element lists and MPI sync on coupled patch edges; TrackingData templates. | M (1-2 wk) for the type; the wave driver dominates | `src/mesh/snappyHexMesh/meshRefinement/patchFaceOrientation.H` |
| `pointData` | custom CUDA frontier | pointEdgePoint plus an extra scalar and vector (pointData.H:63-73), updated on squared distance to origin. It is the transport type for both medial-axis waves — the update rule is a simple min-distance comparison, ideal for a GPU frontier. | PointEdgeWave host driver with per-iteration global reduce and MPI point sync; transform() across coupled patches takes a polyPatch& and a transformer&; Istream/Ostream operators. | M (1-2 wk) for the type; the wave driver dominates | `src/mesh/snappyHexMesh/snappyHexMeshDriver/pointData/pointData.H` |
| `pointEdgeCollapse` | custom CUDA frontier | POD transport of (collapsePoint, collapseIndex, priority) with a simple adoption rule — a point takes a neighbour's target when the neighbour has higher priority or a lower index. The rule is a min/max reduction, well suited to a GPU frontier. | Driven by the host PointEdgeWave with its changed-point lists, per-iteration global reduce and MPI sync; carries Istream/Ostream operators and TrackingData templates. | M (1-2 wk) for the type; the wave driver dominates | `src/polyTopoChange/polyTopoChange/pointEdgeCollapse/pointEdgeCollapse.H` |
| `projectCurveEdge` | LBVH/OptiX for the edge-mesh queries + custom CUDA | Nearest-point queries against an extendedEdgeMesh feature curve with pointConstraint accumulation to keep points on the curve. Same batched-query-plus-host-octree shape as projectEdge. | extendedEdgeMesh octree is host-only; pointConstraint rank logic is branchy per point; feature curves are read from .eMesh files at construction. | L (1-2 mo); none for the time loop | `src/mesh/blockMesh/blockEdges/projectCurveEdge/projectCurveEdge.H` |
| `projectEdge` | LBVH/OptiX for findNearest + custom CUDA + Thrust scan for the re-spacing | projectEdge.C:160-260 runs a maxIter=10 fixed-point loop: batched searchableSurfacesQueries::findNearest over the whole lambda list (host indexedOctree recursion), then rebuilds a cumulative-length parameterisation and a linearInterpolationWeights re-spacing, with a global sum(mag(residual)) convergence test each pass. The queries are batched and independent, so the shape is GPU-friendly; the machinery is not. | indexedOctree is a recursive host structure in src/meshTools; the surface is never on device. linearInterpolationWeights allocates labelList/scalarField per point per iteration. OBJstream debug IO in the loop. Global residual reduction each pass. | L (1-2 mo) and only as part of a full GPU meshing stack; none for a device-resident solve loop | `src/mesh/blockMesh/blockEdges/projectEdge/projectEdge.H` |
| `projectFace` | LBVH/OptiX for findNearest + custom CUDA | projectFace.C (367 lines) alternates i-then-j prediction/projection sweeps: normalised edge lengths give (u,v), findNearest projects, then constant-j and constant-i lines are re-interpolated to keep the parameterisation smooth, iterated to relTol/absTol. The projection is a batched independent query; the re-spacing sweeps are 1-D scans along grid lines. | Host indexedOctree; the alternating i/j sweeps serialise along each grid line (though lines are independent); convergence test needs a global reduce per sweep. | L (1-2 mo) inside a GPU mesher; none for the time loop | `src/mesh/blockMesh/blockFaces/projectFace/projectFace.H` |
| `refinementData` | custom CUDA frontier + CUB compaction | The type itself is two labels (refinementCount_, count_) — device-copyable POD with a cheap update rule. The difficulty is entirely in the FaceCellWave driver that consumes it: an iterative frontier propagation over faces and cells until no change. | FaceCellWave lives in src/meshTools and is a host loop with changedFaces/changedCells lists, per-iteration global reduction and MPI boundary exchange; the transport type also carries Istream/Ostream operators and TrackingData templates that do not survive device compilation. | M (1-2 wk) for the transport type; the wave driver is the real cost | `src/polyTopoChange/polyTopoChange/refinementData.H` |
| `refinementDistanceData` | custom CUDA frontier + CUB compaction | Transports a nearest high-level origin point plus level0 buffer size; wantedLevel(pt) drops one level per level0Size*nBufferLayers of distance walked. Same POD-transport-plus-host-wave structure as refinementData, with a vector payload. | Same FaceCellWave host driver, per-iteration reduction and MPI exchange. | M (1-2 wk) for the type; wave driver dominates | `src/polyTopoChange/polyTopoChange/refinementDistanceData.H` |
| `refinementFeatures` | custom LBVH over edge segments | refinementFeatures.C has 22 findNearest/findLine sites over extendedEdgeMesh octrees (findNearestEdge/findNearestPoint), plus per-feature (distance, level) tables. Nearest-segment queries batch well. | Edge-mesh octree is host-recursive; .eMesh files read at construction; results feed the order-dependent markForRefine budget. | L (1-2 mo); none for the time loop | `src/mesh/snappyHexMesh/refinementFeatures/refinementFeatures.H` |
| `refinementRegions` | custom LBVH inside/outside + distance queries | findLevel answers 'what level should this point have' by inside/outside test or nearest-distance interpolation of a (distance -> level) table; span modes interpolate a surface-closeness field. Per-query independent. | Host searchableSurface octrees; insideSpan/outsideSpan needs a precomputed span field from a separate wave; 676 lines built around host Lists. | L (1-2 mo); none for the time loop | `src/mesh/snappyHexMesh/refinementRegions/refinementRegions.H` |
| `refinementSurfaces` | OptiX / custom LBVH + Thrust | refinementSurfaces.C is 1240 lines with 20 findNearest/findLine sites — findNearestIntersection and findAllIntersections against the searchableSurfaces set, plus a per-global-region level lookup. This is a ray/nearest-point acceleration structure, which is the one thing in this subsystem a GPU is genuinely built for. | indexedOctree is a recursive pointer structure in src/meshTools built and traversed on the host; would need replacing wholesale with an LBVH. 9 Map<> sites for region->level lookup. Never touched after meshing. | XL (quarter+) to replace the search engine; none for the time loop | `src/mesh/snappyHexMesh/refinementSurfaces/refinementSurfaces.H` |
| `shrinkMeshMedialDistance` | custom CUDA frontier (medial wave) + smoothing kernels | snappyLayerDriverShrink.C:1348-1834. The core scaling d_wall/(d_wall + d_medial) is elementwise, but it is wrapped in a PointEdgeWave (line 1591), repeated averageNeighbours smoothing (1652, 1676) and a quality-controlled scale-back through motionSmoother. | PointEdgeWave host driver; meshCheck::checkMesh returning labelHashSet inside the relaxation loop; syncTools::getMasterPoints/getMasterEdges (1381-1382) and 13 Pstream sites. | L (1-2 mo) inside a GPU mesher; none for the time loop | `src/mesh/snappyHexMesh/snappyHexMeshDriver/snappyLayerDriverShrink.C` |
| `trackedParticle` | custom CUDA barycentric tracking + CUB compaction | trackedParticle.C is only 231 lines: standard barycentric tracking from edge start to edge end, writing max(level) into each visited cell. Per-particle independent and a well-understood GPU workload. | Lives in a Cloud<> which is an intrusive doubly-linked list (IDLList) of heap-allocated particles with MPI transfer at processor patches; the max-into-cell write needs atomics; tracking requires tet decomposition addressing. | M (1-2 wk) for the tracking kernel, L for the cloud infrastructure; none for the time loop | `src/mesh/snappyHexMesh/trackedParticle/trackedParticle.H` |

## Mesh: Mesh tools and searching

### A-trivial  <sub>(32)</sub>

| Component | Library | Why | Blockers | Effort | Path |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PatchEdgeFacePointData` | none | patchEdgeFacePoint with an extra payload datum carried alongside the winning origin. Device-trivial as long as the payload Type is itself POD (scalar, vector, tensor) - which is the normal usage. | If instantiated with a container-valued payload it stops being POD and becomes D-hard, exactly as wallFace does. The atomic-update-of-a-compound-struct problem grows with the payload size. | S (~1 d) for POD payloads | `src/meshTools/algorithms/PatchEdgeFaceWave/PatchEdgeFacePointData.H` |
| `WallInfo` | none | A templated wrapper that adds a transported payload to a wall-location type. It contributes no algorithm of its own - it forwards update() to the wrapped location and carries the datum. POD if both halves are POD. | Inherits the POD-ness of its template arguments: WallInfo<wallPoint> is fine, WallInfo<wallFace> is not. Needs __host__ __device__ on all forwarding members. | S (~1 d) | `src/meshTools/patchDist/WallInfo/WallInfo.H` |
| `WallLocationData` | none | Origin + squared distance + an arbitrary datum carried from the winning origin. Device-trivial for scalar/vector/tensor data, which is the normal instantiation. | Same template POD constraint as WallInfo. The larger the datum, the harder the concurrent winner-take-all update - past ~16 bytes prefer a two-pass (argmin then gather) formulation over CAS. | S (~1 d) | `src/meshTools/patchDist/WallLocation/WallLocationData.H` |
| `cellInfo` | none | A single cell-type flag (CUT/OUTSIDE/INSIDE) that propagates unless the receiving cell is CUT, which blocks the wave. One byte of state and one branch - device-trivial as a payload. | Only the wave engine, and its consumer cellClassification is host-only meshing code, so there is little reason to port it. | S (~1 d) | `src/meshTools/cellClassification/cellInfo.H` |
| `labelBits` | none | A single 4-byte POD wrapping `(val<<3)\|bits` with val()=data_>>3 and bits()=data_&7. Every accessor is already inline and branch-free. Annotate __host__ __device__ and it works verbatim as the octree's child-slot encoding on device. | The pack() FULLDEBUG branch calls FatalErrorInFunction - must be #ifdef'd out for device compilation. Assumes 32-bit label; with WM_LABEL_SIZE=64 the 29-bit comment is stale but the shift still works. | S (~1 h) | `src/OpenFOAM/algorithms/indexedOctree/labelBits.H` |
| `labelledTri` | none | A triFace plus a region label - four labels, 16 bytes with 32-bit labels, POD and naturally aligned. This is the triangle record you upload to a device BVH verbatim. Annotate the accessors __host__ __device__ and it is done. | The Istream/Ostream operators are host-only; keep them out of device-visible code. With 64-bit labels the struct doubles to 32 bytes, which hurts bandwidth in a BVH leaf - consider a narrowed device-side triangle type. | S (~1 h) | `src/triSurface/tools/labelledTri/labelledTri.H` |
| `minData` | none | A single passive label with update: data = min(data, neighbourData). One int, one atomicMin. The ideal FaceCellWave payload and the reason regionSplit's merge phase is the tractable part of that class. | Only the wave engine. | S (~1 d) | `src/meshTools/regionSplit/minData.H` |
| `momentOfInertia` | CUB/Thrust (segmented reduction) | Genuinely device-friendly and genuinely loop-relevant (sixDoF rigid-body motion evaluates body inertia as the body moves). Verified: the implementation is a bare forAll over triFaces (momentOfInertia.C:85,206,230) accumulating Mirtich's ten volume integrals into scalars - i.e. a reduction over a 10-wide struct, which CUB does directly. meshInertia does the same per cell over cellTets (line 334), a segmented reduce. No hash sets, no dynamic lists, no sorting anywhere in the file. | The cell tet decomposition (cellTets) must be precomputed as a CSR and uploaded. The ten integrals are accumulated in a fixed order on host; a tree reduction reorders them, so expect last-bits differences in the inertia tensor - use compensated summation if the rigid-body integrator is sensitive. applyParallelAxisTheorem is a 9-FLOP postscript. | S-M (~1 wk) | `src/meshTools/momentOfInertia/momentOfInertia.H` |
| `patchEdgeFacePoint` | none | A POD pair (point origin, scalar distSqr) whose update() keeps whichever origin has the smaller tolerance-guarded magSqr. Four scalars, a handful of FLOPs; annotate __host__ __device__ and it is done. | Useless until PatchEdgeFaceWave is ported. The winner-take-all update across concurrent edges needs an atomic min on the (distSqr, origin) pair rather than a plain store. | S (~1 d) | `src/meshTools/algorithms/PatchEdgeFaceWave/patchEdgeFacePoint.H` |
| `patchEdgeFaceRegion` | none | A single label whose update takes min(region) over incoming values, with -2 marking blocked elements. That is exactly atomicMin on an int - the best possible case for a concurrent wave update, with no CAS loop needed. | Only the wave engine. The -2 blocked sentinel must be handled before the atomicMin so blocked elements are not pulled below the valid range. | S (~1 d) | `src/meshTools/algorithms/PatchEdgeFaceWave/patchEdgeFaceRegion.H` |
| `pointEdgeDist` | none | POD nearest-origin point plus distSqr = magSqr(p - p_origin). Four scalars, one comparison - identical in shape to wallPoint and equally device-friendly. | Only PointEdgeWave. Same winner-take-all atomic consideration as wallPoint. | S (~1 d) | `src/meshTools/pointDist/pointEdgeDist.H` |
| `pointEdgePoint` | none | POD: nearest wall origin point plus distSqr = magSqr(x - x_origin), with the update accepting a candidate only when it reduces distSqr beyond a tolerance. Four scalars, device-safe verbatim. | Only PointEdgeWave. The tolerance-guarded accept makes the update non-idempotent under concurrent execution, so the atomic must compare on distSqr and swap the whole struct - a 64-bit key + index formulation is cleaner than CAS-ing 4 scalars. | S (~1 d) | `src/meshTools/algorithms/PointEdgeWave/pointEdgePoint.H` |
| `pointToPointPlanarInterpolation` | none (or cuSPARSE if you prefer to express it as an SpMV) | The hot path is genuinely trivial and genuinely per-timestep (timeVaryingMappedFixedValue BCs evaluate it every step). Verified in pointToPointPlanarInterpolationTemplates.C:44-61: interpolate() is a forAll over the destination points doing a fixed 3-point gather - FixedList<label,3> nearestVertex_ and FixedList<scalar,3> nearestVertexWeight_ per target, with -1 sentinels collapsing to 2-point or 1-point cases. That is a 3-nonzero-per-row SpMV with a compile-time-known row length: one coalesced-ish kernel, no atomics, no allocation. | The WEIGHTS come from a host-side least-squares plane fit plus a geompack Delaunay triangulation (calcWeights) - keep that on the host and upload the two FixedList arrays once. The -1 sentinel branching causes minor divergence; you can eliminate it by storing a zero weight and a repeated index instead. Weights must be rebuilt (host) if the source point set changes, which for a moving source surface means a per-step round trip - check that first. | S (~2-3 d) | `src/meshTools/triSurface/triSurfaceTools/pointToPointPlanarInterpolation.H` |
| `pointTopoDistanceData` | none | The PointEdgeWave analogue: an integer topological distance incremented per point->edge->point step. Idempotent atomicMin, no floating-point tolerance anywhere. | Only PointEdgeWave. | S (~1 d) | `src/meshTools/meshStructure/pointTopoDistanceData.H` |
| `polyCellSet` | none (device index array) | The one selection class that IS on the time-loop path - fvModels and fvConstraints apply their sources over cells() every single iteration. What they consume is a plain labelList (polyCellSet.C:82,91) or, for `all`, an identity map (line 127). Upload that index array once and every source kernel uses it as a gather map. Selection happens only in setCells() at construction and on topoChange. | setCells() itself uses a labelHashSet plus toc() (line 62-82) and, for `select points`, meshSearch::findCell - keep both on the host. For `select all`, do NOT materialise the identity map on device; carry a flag and skip the indirection so the kernel stays a contiguous stride-1 read. topoChange invalidates the array and forces a re-upload. | S (~2-3 d) | `src/meshTools/sets/polyCellSet/polyCellSet.H` |
| `searchableBox` | none | Closed-form slab intersection plus per-component clamp to [min,max]. No allocation, no addressing, no branching beyond the six face tests. Six scalars of state fit in __constant__ memory or kernel arguments. | Only the virtual dispatch of the searchableSurface base - inline the math directly into the calling kernel. | S (~1-2 d) | `src/meshTools/searchableSurfaces/searchableBox/searchableBox.H` |
| `searchableCylinder` | none | Radial distance \|(p-p1) - ((p-p1)&a)a\| = r with an axial clamp, and a quadratic solve for line intersection. Pure arithmetic on 7 scalars of state; identical cost per thread so no divergence. | Virtual dispatch only. Watch the degenerate \|p-p1\| ~ 0 branch and rootVSmall guards under fast-math. | S (~1-2 d) | `src/meshTools/searchableSurfaces/searchableCylinder/searchableCylinder.H` |
| `searchableDisk` | none | Plane projection ((p-o)&n)=0 then a radial clamp to r, and a ray-plane hit with a radius test. Seven scalars of state, no allocation, uniform per-thread cost. | Virtual dispatch only. | S (~1-2 d) | `src/meshTools/searchableSurfaces/searchableDisk/searchableDisk.H` |
| `searchablePlane` | none | Signed distance d = (p-p0)&n and a line hit at t = -d/(n&(e-s)). Six scalars of state, three FLOPs. The cheapest device kernel in the whole subsystem. | Virtual dispatch only. The planeType parsing (pointAndNormal / planeEquation / embeddedPoints) is host dictionary work at construction. | S (~1 d) | `src/meshTools/searchableSurfaces/searchablePlane/searchablePlane.H` |
| `searchablePlate` | none | Plane projection then clamp the two in-plane components to [origin, origin+span]. The 'exactly one zero span component' constraint is validated once on the host, so the device kernel is branch-free given a precomputed normal direction index. | Virtual dispatch only. | S (~1 d) | `src/meshTools/searchableSurfaces/searchablePlate/searchablePlate.H` |
| `searchableSphere` | none | Read the source: findNearest (searchableSphere.C:42-67) is c + r*(p-c)/\|p-c\| with a magN<rootVSmall guard, and the batched overload (line 205) is a bare forAll over samples with zero allocation in the loop body. No allocation, no hashing, no sorting in the hot path. | (1) mesh().edges() must be a device array of (start,end) label pairs. ### D-hard  <sub>(25)</sub>

| Component | Library | Why | Blockers | Effort | Path |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `FaceCellWave` | CUB (DeviceSelect::Flagged for frontier compaction, DeviceReduce for convergence) | The single most important component in this subsystem for a device-resident loop: it drives wallDist (fvPatchDistWave), fvcSmooth/fvcSpread (VoF, every step), LES smoothDelta, and meshPhiCorrect in the moving-mesh stitcher. Structurally it is a level-synchronous BFS: PackedBoolList changedFace_/changedCell_ plus DynamicList<label> changedFaces_/changedCells_ frontiers (FaceCellWave.H:110-119), alternating face->cell and cell->face sweeps. That maps well: bitmask as uint32 + atomicOr, frontier via atomic append or CUB DeviceSelect, one kernel per half-sweep. | (1) reduce(totNChanged, sumOp<label>()) at FaceCellWave.C:866,943 controls the outer loop - a 4-byte D2H per sweep unless you move loop control into a CUDA graph or a device-side while. (2) The templated Type must be POD and __device__-constructible: true for wallPoint/topoDistanceData/minData/smoothData, FALSE for wallFace (holds a pointField). (3) Type::updateCell/updateFace are host member templates taking TrackingData& - all need __host__ __device__. (4) Multiple faces update the same cell, so allCellInfo_[celli] writes race - needs an atomic min on a (distSqr, origin) pair (64-bit key CAS) or a two-pass gather reformulation. (5) PstreamBuffers nonBlocking exchange (line 383) and the explicitConnections_ baffle DynamicLists (lines 543-547) - the MPI half vanishes at one rank, the baffle half does not. (6) transformer application per cyclic patch. | L-XL (~2-3 mo for a validated device wave) | `src/meshTools/algorithms/FaceCellWave/FaceCellWave.H` |
| `PatchEdgeFaceWave` | CUB | Identical machinery restricted to a patch surface: PackedBoolList changedEdge_/changedFace_ plus DynamicList<label> changedEdges_/changedFaces_ (PatchEdgeFaceWave.H:100-109), with returnReduce(changedEdges_.size(), sumOp<label>()) at PatchEdgeFaceWave.C:541. The saving grace is scale - a patch is O(n^2/3) of the mesh, so even a mediocre device implementation is cheap, and most uses (regionToFace, patchPatchDist) are preprocessing. | globalMeshData coupled-edge synchronisation (line 171) plus PatchTools addressing; patchEdges_/coupledEdges_/sameEdgeOrientation_ must be precomputed. Same per-sweep reduction sync and POD-payload constraints. Low arithmetic intensity on a small working set means launch overhead may dominate - consider leaving it on host. | M-L (~1 mo) | `src/meshTools/algorithms/PatchEdgeFaceWave/PatchEdgeFaceWave.H` |
| `PointEdgeWave` | CUB (frontier compaction and reduction) | Same frontier structure as FaceCellWave but over points and edges, alternating point->edge and edge->point updates. Used by pointDist and by the multiValveEngine piston point data, i.e. it is on the moving-mesh loop path. On one rank the two-stage parallel exchange (patch points in (patchFace,index) notation, then a combineReduce over globally shared points) disappears entirely. | reduce(totNChanged, sumOp<label>()) at PointEdgeWave.C:516,756,824 forces a per-sweep sync. DynamicList patchInfo/thisPoints/nbrPoints (lines 256-258, 358-360) for the exchange. Point-centred scatter has much higher contention than the face variant - a point is touched by every incident edge - so atomic pressure is worse. Same POD/__device__ constraints on Type as FaceCellWave. | L (~1-2 mo, given FaceCellWave is done first) | `src/meshTools/algorithms/PointEdgeWave/PointEdgeWave.H` |
| `closedTriSurfaceMesh` | none (inherits triSurfaceMesh) | Identical to triSurfaceMesh except hasVolumeType() is forced true so inside/outside queries are permitted on a surface with small holes. Zero additional device work once triSurfaceMesh is ported - it is a one-line override. | Same as triSurfaceMesh. The forced-closed assertion makes the volumeType result unreliable near the imperfections, which on device shows up as nondeterministic-looking classification at those spots - not a porting bug. | none beyond triSurfaceMesh (~1 d) | `src/meshTools/searchableSurfaces/closedTriSurfaceMesh/closedTriSurfaceMesh.H` |
| `indexedOctree` | none (custom CUDA BVH traversal kernels); CUB for the two-pass hit compaction | Split it in two. The QUERY side is a static-tree BVH traversal and is genuinely portable: node is POD (treeBoundBox = 2 points, label parent_, FixedList<labelBits,8>), findNearest recurses only to maxLevels (default ~10) so an explicit 10-deep register stack suffices, and findLine is already STACKLESS (walkToNeighbour/walkToParent, indexedOctree.C:994-1281) which is ideal for a warp. The BUILD side (divide/splitNodes/compactContents, indexedOctree.C:112-341) runs on DynamicList<node> + DynamicList<labelList> with repeated reallocation and in-place list mutation (the comment at line 234 warns it loops over the same DynamicList it modifies) - keep that on the host, build once at startup, upload, never rebuild in the loop. | contents_ is a labelListList (List<labelList>, one heap buffer per leaf) - must be flattened to a CSR (offsets,values) pair before upload. findBox/findSphere (indexedOctree.C:1762-1856) accumulate into a labelHashSet - must become count-pass + prefix-sum + fill-pass, or an atomic append into a bounded per-query buffer. nodeTypes_ is a `mutable PackedList<2>` lazily populated by the recursive calcVolumeType on first getVolumeType call - a data race if left lazy on device, so force it on the host before upload. perturbTol_ is a static scalar (needs __constant__).
